Testimony 4 says that Ms. Crane (Rosie) was attacked by Kay Faraday on the viewing platform. Testimony 3 mentions that the only exit from the storeroom is a hatch leading to the viewing platform.

The storeroom's exit is via a hatch to the viewing platform (Testimony 3). Looking at the rules, Rule 7 says if XXX connects to YYY via a hatch, then YYY is beneath XXX. If the storeroom connects to the viewing platform via a hatch, then the viewing platform is beneath the storeroom. But if the attack was in the storeroom, then the location of the attack isn't the viewing platform as per the testimony.

First, looking at the testimonies from Andrews. She says she discovered the body. Testimony 5 states that she knocked over the flower vase, which fell onto the guitar case. Now, let's check the evidence related to the guitar case. Evidence 7 mentions that the guitar case was found next to the victim, empty, with water only on top of the lid and only Corrida's fingerprints. Also, Rule 8 says if the vase is smashed, then water is on the guitar case's lid. The crime photo (Evidence 9) describes a smashed vase next to the guitar case, which would imply water on the lid. But if the vase was smashed and the water is on the lid, that's consistent with Rule 8. But wait, the testimony says Andrews knocked over the vase, causing it to fall onto the guitar case. If Andrews knocked the vase over, wouldn't her fingerprints be on the guitar case? But the guitar case has only Corrida's fingerprints. That's a contradiction. Because if she knocked the vase over onto the guitar case, she might have touched it, but the guitar case only has Corrida's fingerprints. 

So, Testimony 5 (Andrews knocked the vase over onto the guitar case) contradicts Evidence 7 (guitar case has only Corrida's fingerprints). Let's check the rules. Rule 15 says if the guitar case has only Corrida's fingerprints, then no one else touched it after the murder. If the vase was knocked over by Andrews after the murder, that would mean she touched the guitar case, which would contradict Rule 15. Therefore, the testimony that she knocked the vase over (which would have required her to touch the guitar case) conflicts with the evidence that the guitar case only has Corrida's fingerprints. So the contradiction is between Testimony 5 and Evidence 7.

<think>
Okay, let's tackle this problem step by step. The goal is to find a contradiction between an evidence and a testimony using the provided spatial reasoning rules. 

First, I need to look at the testimonies and see which ones might conflict with the evidence. Let's start with O'Connor's testimonies. He says he saw Juniper dragging a large mat from behind the stage towards him, and he was in a vantage point between the stage and the maintenance area. 

Looking at the evidence, there's the Stage Set Up Photos (Evidence 18) which mention a tall construction screen between the stage and the maintenance area. Also, there's a construction screen mentioned in Rule 14. Rule 14 states that if a construction screen is between YYY and ZZZ, it blocks direct access from YYY to ZZZ. 

So, if there's a construction screen between the stage and the maintenance area (as per Evidence 18), then according to Rule 14, it would block direct access. But O'Connor claims he was in a vantage point between the stage and maintenance area and saw Juniper dragging the mat towards him. If the screen blocks direct access, then Juniper would have to go around it, but O'Connor's vantage point might be obstructed. However, Testimony 1 says he saw her dragging the mat from behind the stage, which is where the screen is. If the screen is there, he shouldn't have a clear line of sight unless the screen wasn't there. 

Wait, but Rule 14 says the screen blocks direct access, not necessarily line of sight. Maybe Rule 1 is more relevant here. Rule 1 says if something is between YYY and ZZZ, it obstructs the line of sight. If the construction screen is between the stage (where Juniper was) and O'Connor's vantage point, then the screen would block the line of sight. But O'Connor claims he saw her, which would contradict the presence of the screen. 

So, Evidence 18 mentions the construction screen between the stage and the vantage point. Testimony 2 says O'Connor was watching from that vantage point. If the screen is there, he shouldn't see her unless the screen wasn't there. Therefore, the existence of the construction screen (Evidence 18) contradicts O'Connor's testimony that he saw Juniper from that vantage point. 

Which rule applies here? Rule 14 is about blocking access, but Rule 1 is about line of sight. Since the issue is about seeing her, Rule 1 would apply. If the construction screen is between the stage and the vantage point, then it obstructs the line of sight. Therefore, O'Connor couldn't have seen her dragging the mat if the screen was there. 

So the contradiction is between Evidence 18 (Stage Set Up Photos) and Testimony 2 (O'Connor's vantage point). The evidence shows the screen is there, which by Rule 1 would block the view, but the testimony says he saw her, implying the screen wasn't there or didn't block the view. That's the contradiction.
